The region shown is <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt4" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwCQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;, &apos;&apos;y&apos;&apos; ∈ [−3,3]"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>x</i>, <i>y</i> ∈ [−3,3]</span>.<br id="mwCg"/>(For <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt5" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwCw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"(&apos;&apos;a&apos;&apos;, &apos;&apos;b&apos;&apos;) = (0, 0)"}},"i":0}}]}'>(<i>a</i>, <i>b</i>) = (0, 0)</span> the function is not smooth and therefore not an elliptic curve.)</figcaption></figure>

<li><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loop_group" title="Loop group">Loop</a></li></ul>
<div style="display:inline-block; padding:0.2em 0.4em; line-height:1.2em; "><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infinite_dimensional_Lie_group" title="Infinite dimensional Lie group" class="mw-redirect">Infinite dimensional Lie group</a> <div class="hlist"><ul><li>O(∞)</li><li>SU(∞)</li><li>Sp(∞)</li></ul></div></div></div></div></td>
</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-content">
<div class="sidebar-list mw-collapsible"><div class="sidebar-list-title" style="background:transparent;border-top:1px solid #aaa;text-align:center;;color: var(--color-base)"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebraic_group" title="Algebraic group">Algebraic groups</a></div><div class="sidebar-list-content mw-collapsible-content hlist" style="border-top:1px solid #aaa;border-bottom:1px solid #aaa;">

</tr><tr><td class="sidebar-navbar"><span class="mw-empty-elt"><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1374427404" about="#mwt22" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Hlist/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1239400231" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" about="#mwt23" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Navbar/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'>.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:inline;font-size:88%;font-weight:normal}.mw-parser-output .navbar-collapse{float:left;text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .navbar-boxtext{word-spacing:0}.mw-parser-output .navbar ul{display:inline-block;white-space:nowrap;line-height:inherit}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::before{margin-right:-0.125em;content:"[ "}.mw-parser-output .navbar-brackets::after{margin-left:-0.125em;content:" ]"}.mw-parser-output .navbar print{.mw-parser-output .navbar{display:none!important}}</style></span><div class="navbar plainlinks hlist navbar-mini"><ul><li class="nv-view"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template:Group_theory_sidebar" title="Template:Group theory sidebar"><abbr title="View this template">v</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-talk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk:Group_theory_sidebar" title="Template talk:Group theory sidebar"><abbr title="Discuss this template">t</abbr></a></li><li class="nv-edit"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special:EditPage/Template:Group_theory_sidebar" title="Special:EditPage/Template:Group theory sidebar"><abbr title="Edit this template">e</abbr></a></li></ul></div></td></tr></tbody></table>

<p id="mwDg">In <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mathematics" title="Mathematics" id="mwDw">mathematics</a>, an <b id="mwEA">elliptic curve</b> is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smoothness" title="Smoothness" id="mwEQ">smooth</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Projective_variety" title="Projective variety" id="mwEg">projective</a>, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebraic_curve" title="Algebraic curve" id="mwEw">algebraic curve</a> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genus_of_an_algebraic_curve" title="Genus of an algebraic curve" class="mw-redirect" id="mwFA">genus</a> one, on which there is a specified point <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt24" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwFQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"O"}},"i":0}}]}'>O</span>. An elliptic curve is defined over a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_(mathematics)" title="Field (mathematics)" id="mwFg">field</a> <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt25" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwFw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span> and describes points in <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt26" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwGA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;K&apos;&apos;{{isup|2}}"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>K</i><span style="padding-left:0.12em;"><sup>2</sup></span></span>, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cartesian_product" title="Cartesian product" id="mwGQ">Cartesian product</a> of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt27" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwGg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span> with itself. If the field's <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Characteristic_(algebra)" title="Characteristic (algebra)" id="mwGw">characteristic</a> is different from 2 and 3, then the curve can be described as a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plane_algebraic_curve" title="Plane algebraic curve" class="mw-redirect" id="mwHA">plane algebraic curve</a> which consists of solutions <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt28" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwHQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"(&apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;, &apos;&apos;y&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'>(<i>x</i>, <i>y</i>)</span> for:</p>

<dl id="mwHg"><dd id="mwHw"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt29" id="mwIA" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"y^2 = x^3 + ax + b"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle y^{2}=x^{3}+ax+b}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <msup>
          <mi>y</mi>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>2</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <msup>
          <mi>x</mi>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>3</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
        <mo>+</mo>
        <mi>a</mi>
        <mi>x</mi>
        <mo>+</mo>
        <mi>b</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle y^{2}=x^{3}+ax+b}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3dbe6cab1bc2c7f1c99757dc6e5d7a517cf9b4f8" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:16.935ex; height:3.009ex;" alt="{\displaystyle y^{2}=x^{3}+ax+b}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwIQ">for some coefficients <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt30" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwIg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"a"}},"i":0}}]}'>a</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt31" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwIw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"b"}},"i":0}}]}'>b</span> in <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt32" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwJA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span>. The curve is required to be <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singular_point_of_a_curve" title="Singular point of a curve" id="mwJQ">non-singular</a>, which means that the curve has no <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cusp_(singularity)" title="Cusp (singularity)" id="mwJg">cusps</a> or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-intersection" title="Self-intersection" class="mw-redirect" id="mwJw">self-intersections</a>. (This is equivalent to the condition <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt33" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwKA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"4&apos;&apos;a&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>3&lt;/sup> + 27&apos;&apos;b&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>2&lt;/sup> ≠ 0"}},"i":0}}]}'>4<i>a</i><sup>3</sup> + 27<i>b</i><sup>2</sup> ≠ 0</span>, that is, being <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Square-free_polynomial" title="Square-free polynomial" id="mwKQ">square-free</a> in <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt34" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwKg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x"}},"i":0}}]}'>x</span>.)  It is usually understood that the curve is embedded in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Projective_plane" title="Projective plane" id="mwKw">projective plane</a>, with the point <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt35" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwLA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"O"}},"i":0}}]}'>O</span> being the unique <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Point_at_infinity" title="Point at infinity" id="mwLQ">point at infinity</a>. Many sources define an elliptic curve to be simply a curve given by an equation of this form. (When the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coefficient_field" title="Coefficient field" class="mw-redirect" id="mwLg">coefficient field</a> has characteristic 2 or 3, the above equation is not quite general enough to include all non-singular <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cubic_plane_curve" title="Cubic plane curve" class="mw-redirect" id="mwLw">cubic curves</a>; see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="#Elliptic_curves_over_a_general_field" about="#mwt36" typeof="mw:Transclusion" class="mw-selflink-fragment" id="mwMA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"section link","href":"./Template:Section_link"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"#Elliptic curves over a general field"}},"i":0}}]}'>§<span typeof="mw:Entity"> </span>Elliptic curves over a general field</a> below.)</p>

<p id="mwMQ">An elliptic curve is an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abelian_variety" title="Abelian variety" id="mwMg">abelian variety</a> – that is, it has a group law defined algebraically, with respect to which it is an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abelian_group" title="Abelian group" id="mwMw">abelian group</a> – and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt37" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwNA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"O"}},"i":0}}]}'>O</span> serves as the identity element.</p>

<p id="mwNQ">If <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt38" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwNg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>2&lt;/sup> = &apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y</i><sup>2</sup> = <i>P</i>(<i>x</i>)</span>, where <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt39" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwNw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> is any polynomial of degree three in <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt40" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwOA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x"}},"i":0}}]}'>x</span> with no repeated roots, the solution set is a nonsingular plane curve of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Genus_(mathematics)" title="Genus (mathematics)" id="mwOQ">genus</a> one, an elliptic curve. If <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt41" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwOg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> has degree four and is <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Square-free_polynomial" title="Square-free polynomial" id="mwOw">square-free</a> this equation again describes a plane curve of genus one; however, it has no natural choice of identity element. More generally, any algebraic curve of genus one, for example the intersection of two <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quadric_(algebraic_geometry)" title="Quadric (algebraic geometry)" id="mwPA">quadric surfaces</a> embedded in three-dimensional projective space, is called an elliptic curve, provided that it is equipped with a marked point to act as the identity.</p>

<p id="mwPQ">Using the theory of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic_function" title="Elliptic function" id="mwPg">elliptic functions</a>, it can be shown that elliptic curves defined over the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_number" title="Complex number" id="mwPw">complex numbers</a> correspond to embeddings of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torus" title="Torus" id="mwQA">torus</a> into the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_projective_plane" title="Complex projective plane" id="mwQQ">complex projective plane</a>. The torus is also an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abelian_group" title="Abelian group" id="mwQg">abelian group</a>, and this correspondence is also a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_isomorphism" title="Group isomorphism" id="mwQw">group isomorphism</a>.</p>

<p id="mwRA">Elliptic curves are especially important in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Number_theory" title="Number theory" id="mwRQ">number theory</a>, and constitute a major area of current research; for example, they were used in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wiles's_proof_of_Fermat's_Last_Theorem" title="Wiles's proof of Fermat's Last Theorem" id="mwRg">Andrew Wiles's proof of Fermat's Last Theorem</a>. They also find applications in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic_curve_cryptography" title="Elliptic curve cryptography" class="mw-redirect" id="mwRw">elliptic curve cryptography</a> (ECC) and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integer_factorization" title="Integer factorization" id="mwSA">integer factorization</a>.</p>

<p id="mwSQ">An elliptic curve is <i id="mwSg">not</i> an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ellipse" title="Ellipse" id="mwSw">ellipse</a> in the sense of a projective conic, which has genus zero: see <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic_integral" title="Elliptic integral" id="mwTA">elliptic integral</a> for the origin of the term. However, there is a natural representation of real elliptic curves with shape invariant <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt42" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwTQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;j&apos;&apos; ≥ 1"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>j</i> ≥ 1</span> as ellipses in the hyperbolic plane <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt44" id="mwTg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\mathbb{H}^2"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <msup>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mi mathvariant="double-struck">H</mi>
          </mrow>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>2</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/fd949bba96edbb143fd05b712e274aa07ab1c75f" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:2.862ex; height:2.676ex;" alt="{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}"/></span>. Specifically, the intersections of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minkowski_hyperboloid" title="Minkowski hyperboloid" class="mw-redirect" id="mwTw">Minkowski hyperboloid</a> with quadric surfaces characterized by a certain constant-angle property produce the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steiner_ellipse" title="Steiner ellipse" id="mwUA">Steiner ellipses</a> in <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt45" id="mwUQ" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\mathbb{H}^2"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <msup>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mi mathvariant="double-struck">H</mi>
          </mrow>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>2</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/fd949bba96edbb143fd05b712e274aa07ab1c75f" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:2.862ex; height:2.676ex;" alt="{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}"/></span> (generated by orientation-preserving collineations). Further, the orthogonal trajectories of these ellipses comprise the elliptic curves with <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt43" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwUg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;j&apos;&apos; ≤ 1"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>j</i> ≤ 1</span>, and any ellipse in <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt46" id="mwUw" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\mathbb{H}^2"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <msup>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mi mathvariant="double-struck">H</mi>
          </mrow>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>2</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/fd949bba96edbb143fd05b712e274aa07ab1c75f" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:2.862ex; height:2.676ex;" alt="{\displaystyle \mathbb {H} ^{2}}"/></span> described as a locus relative to two foci is uniquely the elliptic curve sum of two Steiner ellipses, obtained by adding the pairs of intersections on each orthogonal trajectory. Here, the vertex of the hyperboloid serves as the identity on each trajectory curve.<sup about="#mwt49" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-1" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-1"}}'><a href="#cite_note-1" id="mwVA"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwVQ"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVg">[</span>1<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwVw">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwWA"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Topologically" title="Topologically" class="mw-redirect" id="mwWQ">Topologically</a>, a complex elliptic curve is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torus" title="Torus" id="mwWg">torus</a>, while a complex ellipse is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sphere" title="Sphere" id="mwWw">sphere</a>.</p>

<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" id="mwXA" data-mw='{"autoGenerated":true}'/></section><section data-mw-section-id="1" id="mwXQ" aria-labelledby="Elliptic_curves_over_the_real_numbers"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Elliptic_curves_over_the_real_numbers">Elliptic curves over the real numbers</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Elliptic curves over the real numbers"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Frame" id="mwXg"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ECClines-3.svg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwXw"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ECClines-3.svg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/ECClines-3.svg/500px-ECClines-3.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d0/ECClines-3.svg/960px-ECClines-3.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="335" data-file-height="190" data-file-type="drawing" height="190" width="335" class="mw-file-element" id="mwYA"/></a><figcaption id="mwYQ">Graphs of curves <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt50" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwYg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>2&lt;/sup> = &apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>3&lt;/sup> − &apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y</i><sup>2</sup> = <i>x</i><sup>3</sup> − <i>x</i></span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt51" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwYw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>2&lt;/sup> = &apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>3&lt;/sup> − &apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos; + 1"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y</i><sup>2</sup> = <i>x</i><sup>3</sup> − <i>x</i> + 1</span></figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwZA">Although the formal definition of an elliptic curve requires some background in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebraic_geometry" title="Algebraic geometry" id="mwZQ">algebraic geometry</a>, it is possible to describe some features of elliptic curves over the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Real_number" title="Real number" id="mwZg">real numbers</a> using only introductory <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebra" title="Algebra" id="mwZw">algebra</a> and <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geometry" title="Geometry" id="mwaA">geometry</a>.</p>

<p id="mwaQ">In this context, an elliptic curve is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plane_curve" title="Plane curve" id="mwag">plane curve</a> defined by an equation of the form</p>

<dl id="mwaw"><dd id="mwbA"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt52" id="mwbQ" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"y^2 = x^3 + ax + b"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle y^{2}=x^{3}+ax+b}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <msup>
          <mi>y</mi>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>2</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <msup>
          <mi>x</mi>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>3</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
        <mo>+</mo>
        <mi>a</mi>
        <mi>x</mi>
        <mo>+</mo>
        <mi>b</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle y^{2}=x^{3}+ax+b}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3dbe6cab1bc2c7f1c99757dc6e5d7a517cf9b4f8" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:16.935ex; height:3.009ex;" alt="{\displaystyle y^{2}=x^{3}+ax+b}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwbg">after a linear change of variables (<span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt53" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwbw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"a"}},"i":0}}]}'>a</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt54" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwcA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"b"}},"i":0}}]}'>b</span> are real numbers). This type of equation is called a  <i id="mwcQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weierstrass_normal_form" title="Weierstrass normal form" class="mw-redirect" id="mwcg">Weierstrass normal form</a></i>, <i id="mwcw">Weierstrass form</i>, or <i id="mwdA">Weierstrass equation</i>.</p>

<p id="mwdQ">The definition of elliptic curve also requires that the curve be <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Singular_point_of_an_algebraic_variety" title="Singular point of an algebraic variety" id="mwdg">non-singular</a>. Geometrically, this means that the graph has no <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cusp_(singularity)" title="Cusp (singularity)" id="mwdw">cusps</a>, self-intersections, or <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isolated_point" title="Isolated point" id="mweA">isolated points</a>. Algebraically, this holds if and only if the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Discriminant" title="Discriminant" id="mweQ">discriminant</a>, <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt55" id="mweg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\Delta"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \Delta }">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi mathvariant="normal">Δ<!-- Δ --></mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \Delta }</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/32769037c408874e1890f77554c65f39c523ebe2" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:1.936ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle \Delta }"/></span>, is not equal to zero, where <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt56" id="mwew" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\Delta"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \Delta }">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi mathvariant="normal">Δ<!-- Δ --></mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle \Delta }</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/32769037c408874e1890f77554c65f39c523ebe2" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:1.936ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle \Delta }"/></span> is defined as:</p>

<dl id="mwfA"><dd id="mwfQ"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt57" id="mwfg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\Delta = -16\\left(4a^3 + 27b^2\\right)"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \Delta =-16\left(4a^{3}+27b^{2}\right)}">

<p id="mwgQ">The discriminant is zero when <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt58" id="mwgg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"a=-3k^2, b=2k^3"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle a=-3k^{2},b=2k^{3}}">

<p id="mwhg">Although the factor −16 is irrelevant to whether or not the curve is non-singular, this definition of the discriminant is useful in a more advanced study of elliptic curves.<sup about="#mwt63" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-2" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-2"}}'><a href="#cite_note-2" id="mwhw"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwiA"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwiQ">[</span>2<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwig">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwiw">The real graph of a non-singular curve has <i id="mwjA">two</i> components if its discriminant is positive, and <i id="mwjQ">one</i> component if it is negative. For example, in the graphs shown in figure to the right, the discriminant in the first case is 64, and in the second case is −368.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="2" id="mwjg" aria-labelledby="Group_law"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Group_law">Group law</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: Group law"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwjw">When working in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Projective_plane" title="Projective plane" id="mwkA">projective plane</a>, the equation in <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homogeneous_coordinates" title="Homogeneous coordinates" id="mwkQ">homogeneous coordinates</a> becomes</p>

      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle ZY^{2}=X^{3}+aZ^{2}X+bZ^{3}.}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/e03918ee5412ec1ab69b6e0c9cf22dd7b8ba92f7" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.505ex; width:26.845ex; height:2.843ex;" alt="{\displaystyle ZY^{2}=X^{3}+aZ^{2}X+bZ^{3}.}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwmw">This resulting equation is defined on the whole projective plane, and the curve it defines projects onto the elliptic curve of interest.  To find its intersection with the line at infinity, we can just posit <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt67" id="mwnA" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"Z = 0"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle Z=0}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>Z</mi>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mn>0</mn>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle Z=0}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/49175243147048b9854d4f1e640e3e0da82c09ff" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:5.941ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle Z=0}"/></span>. This implies <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt68" id="mwnQ" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"X^3 = 0"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle X^{3}=0}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <msup>
          <mi>X</mi>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>3</mn>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mn>0</mn>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle X^{3}=0}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/3ef14a2e2ef0eb207bda55c66ead31bda29cb120" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:7.312ex; height:2.676ex;" alt="{\displaystyle X^{3}=0}"/></span>, which in a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_(mathematics)" title="Field (mathematics)" id="mwng">field</a> means <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt69" id="mwnw" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"X = 0"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle X=0}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>X</mi>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mn>0</mn>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle X=0}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/d519e9e94f279ea82581dfa70a2444e896e2d860" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:6.241ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle X=0}"/></span>. <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt70" id="mwoA" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"Y"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle Y}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>Y</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle Y}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/961d67d6b454b4df2301ac571808a3538b3a6d3f" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.171ex; width:1.773ex; height:2.009ex;" alt="{\displaystyle Y}"/></span> on the other hand can take any value, and thus all triplets <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt71" id="mwoQ" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"(0,Y,0)"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle (0,Y,0)}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
        <mn>0</mn>
        <mo>,</mo>
        <mi>Y</mi>
        <mo>,</mo>
        <mn>0</mn>
        <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle (0,Y,0)}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a2ebd0e1198b117de82d6c953cdc78b0cca855e9" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:7.975ex; height:2.843ex;" alt="{\displaystyle (0,Y,0)}"/></span> satisfy the equation. In projective geometry this set is simply the point <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt72" id="mwog" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"O = [0:1:0]"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle O=[0:1:0]}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>O</mi>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mo stretchy="false">[</mo>
        <mn>0</mn>
        <mo>:</mo>
        <mn>1</mn>
        <mo>:</mo>
        <mn>0</mn>
        <mo stretchy="false">]</mo>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle O=[0:1:0]}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/18273b8a82c7523639ad09fbbd45bd749d2fdb14" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:13.527ex; height:2.843ex;" alt="{\displaystyle O=[0:1:0]}"/></span>, which is thus the unique intersection of the curve with the line at infinity.</p>

<p id="mwow">Since the curve is smooth, hence <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Continuous_function" title="Continuous function" id="mwpA">continuous</a>, it can be shown that this point at infinity is the identity element of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_(mathematics)" title="Group (mathematics)" id="mwpQ">group</a> structure whose operation is geometrically described as follows:</p>

<p id="mwpg">Since the curve is symmetric about the <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt73" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwpw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x"}},"i":0}}]}'>x</span> axis, given any point <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt74" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwqA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span>, we can take <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt75" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwqQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"−&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'>−<i>P</i></span> to be the point opposite it. We then have <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt77" id="mwqg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"-O = O"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle -O=O}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mo>−<!-- − --></mo>
        <mi>O</mi>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mi>O</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle -O=O}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/7cdda9c82d463006b0da1f490e2322bd1004743f" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.505ex; width:8.453ex; height:2.343ex;" alt="{\displaystyle -O=O}"/></span>, as <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt78" id="mwqw" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"O"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle O}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>O</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle O}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9d70e1d0d87e2ef1092ea1ffe2923d9933ff18fc" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:1.773ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle O}"/></span> lies on the <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt76" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwrA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"XZ"}},"i":0}}]}'>XZ</span> plane, so that <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt79" id="mwrQ" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"-O"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle -O}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mo>−<!-- − --></mo>
        <mi>O</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle -O}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/df10fb57bce3d9e2e6a9264bb1b73f13683abb10" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.505ex; width:3.581ex; height:2.343ex;" alt="{\displaystyle -O}"/></span> is also the symmetrical of <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt80" id="mwrg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"O"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle O}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>O</mi>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle O}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/9d70e1d0d87e2ef1092ea1ffe2923d9933ff18fc" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.338ex; width:1.773ex; height:2.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle O}"/></span> about the origin, and thus represents the same projective point.</p>

<p id="mwrw">If <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt81" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwsA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt82" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwsQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Q"}},"i":0}}]}'>Q</span> are two points on the curve, then we can uniquely describe a third point <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt83" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwsg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;Q&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>P</i> + <i>Q</i></span> in the following way. First, draw the line that intersects <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt84" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwsw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt85" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwtA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Q"}},"i":0}}]}'>Q</span>. This will generally intersect the cubic at a third point, <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt86" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwtQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"R"}},"i":0}}]}'>R</span>. We then take <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt87" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwtg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;Q&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>P</i> + <i>Q</i></span> to be <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt88" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwtw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"−&apos;&apos;R&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'>−<i>R</i></span>, the point opposite <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt89" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwuA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"R"}},"i":0}}]}'>R</span>.</p>

<p id="mwuQ">This definition for addition works except in a few special cases related to the point at infinity and intersection multiplicity. The first is when one of the points is <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt90" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwug" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"O"}},"i":0}}]}'>O</span>. Here, we define <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt91" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwuw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;O&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;O&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>P</i> + <i>O</i> = <i>P</i> = <i>O</i> + <i>P</i></span>, making <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt92" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwvA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"O"}},"i":0}}]}'>O</span> the identity of the group. If <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt93" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwvQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;Q&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>P</i> = <i>Q</i></span>, we only have one point, thus we cannot define the line between them. In this case, we use the tangent line to the curve at this point as our line. In most cases, the tangent will intersect a second point <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt94" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwvg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"R"}},"i":0}}]}'>R</span>, and we can take its opposite. If <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt95" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwvw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt96" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwwA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Q"}},"i":0}}]}'>Q</span> are opposites of each other, we define <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt97" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwwQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;Q&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;O&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>P</i> + <i>Q</i> = <i>O</i></span>. Lastly, if <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt98" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwwg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> is an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflection_point" title="Inflection point" id="mwww">inflection point</a> (a point where the concavity of the curve changes), we take <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt99" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwxA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"R"}},"i":0}}]}'>R</span> to be <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt100" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwxQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> itself, and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt101" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwxg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>P</i> + <i>P</i></span> is simply the point opposite itself, i.e. itself.</p>

<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-center" typeof="mw:File" id="mwxw"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ECClines.svg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwyA"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:ECClines.svg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/ECClines.svg/960px-ECClines.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c1/ECClines.svg/1920px-ECClines.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="680" data-file-height="206" data-file-type="drawing" height="206" width="680" class="mw-file-element" id="mwyQ"/></a><figcaption id="mwyg"></figcaption></figure>

<p id="mwyw">Let <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt102" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwzA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span> be a field over which the curve is defined (that is, the coefficients of the defining equation or equations of the curve are in <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt103" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwzQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span>) and denote the curve by <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt104" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwzg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"E"}},"i":0}}]}'>E</span>. Then the <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt105" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwzw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span>-<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rational_point" title="Rational point" id="mw0A">rational points</a> of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt106" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw0Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"E"}},"i":0}}]}'>E</span> are the points on <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt107" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw0g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"E"}},"i":0}}]}'>E</span> whose coordinates all lie in <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt108" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw0w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span>, including the point at infinity. The set of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt109" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw1A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span>-rational points is denoted by <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt110" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw1Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;E&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;K&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>E</i>(<i>K</i>)</span>. <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt111" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw1g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;E&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;K&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>E</i>(<i>K</i>)</span> is a group, because properties of polynomial equations show that if <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt112" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw1w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> is in <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt113" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw2A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;E&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;K&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>E</i>(<i>K</i>)</span>, then <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt114" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw2Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"−&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'>−<i>P</i></span> is also in <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt115" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw2g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;E&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;K&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>E</i>(<i>K</i>)</span>, and if two of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt116" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw2w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span>, <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt117" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw3A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Q"}},"i":0}}]}'>Q</span>, <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt118" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw3Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"R"}},"i":0}}]}'>R</span> are in <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt119" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw3g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;E&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;K&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>E</i>(<i>K</i>)</span>, then so is the third. Additionally, if <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt120" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw3w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span> is a subfield of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt121" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw4A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"L"}},"i":0}}]}'>L</span>, then <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt122" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw4Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;E&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;K&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>E</i>(<i>K</i>)</span> is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Subgroup" title="Subgroup" id="mw4g">subgroup</a> of <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt123" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw4w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;E&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;L&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>E</i>(<i>L</i>)</span>.</p>

<section data-mw-section-id="3" id="mw5A" aria-labelledby="Algebraic_interpretation"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Algebraic_interpretation">Algebraic interpretation</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Algebraic interpretation"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mw5Q">The above groups can be described algebraically as well as geometrically. Given the curve <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt124" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw5g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>2&lt;/sup> = &apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>3&lt;/sup> + &apos;&apos;bx&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;c&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y</i><sup>2</sup> = <i>x</i><sup>3</sup> + <i>bx</i> + <i>c</i></span> over the field <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt125" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw5w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span> (whose <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_subfield" title="Prime subfield" class="mw-redirect" id="mw6A">characteristic</a> we assume to be neither 2 nor 3), and points <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt126" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw6Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos; = (&apos;&apos;x&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;, &apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>P</i> = (<i>x<sub>P</sub></i>, <i>y<sub>P</sub></i>)</span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt127" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw6g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;Q&apos;&apos; = (&apos;&apos;x&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;, &apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>Q</i> = (<i>x<sub>Q</sub></i>, <i>y<sub>Q</sub></i>)</span> on the curve, assume first that <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt128" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw6w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;x&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; ≠ &apos;&apos;x&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>x<sub>P</sub></i> ≠ <i>x<sub>Q</sub></i></span> (case <i id="mw7A">1</i>). Let <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt129" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw7Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;sx&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;d&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y</i> = <i>sx</i> + <i>d</i></span> be the equation of the line that intersects <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt130" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw7g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt131" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw7w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Q"}},"i":0}}]}'>Q</span>, which has the following slope:</p>

<p id="mw8w">The line equation and the curve equation intersect at the points <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt133" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw9A" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>x<sub>P</sub></span>, <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt134" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw9Q" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>x<sub>Q</sub></span>, and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt135" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw9g" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x&lt;sub>R&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>x<sub>R</sub></span>, so the equations have identical <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt136" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mw9w" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"y"}},"i":0}}]}'>y</span> values at these values.</p>

<p id="mw_w">Since <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt139" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>x<sub>P</sub></span>, <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt140" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>x<sub>Q</sub></span>, and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt141" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x&lt;sub>R&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>x<sub>R</sub></span> are solutions, this equation has its roots at exactly the same <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt142" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x"}},"i":0}}]}'>x</span> values as</p>
<dl id="mwAQQ"><dd id="mwAQU"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt143" id="mwAQY" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"(x - x_P) (x - x_Q) (x - x_R) = x^3 + (-x_P - x_Q - x_R) x^2 + (x_P x_Q + x_P x_R + x_Q x_R) x - x_P x_Q x_R,"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle (x-x_{P})(x-x_{Q})(x-x_{R})=x^{3}+(-x_{P}-x_{Q}-x_{R})x^{2}+(x_{P}x_{Q}+x_{P}x_{R}+x_{Q}x_{R})x-x_{P}x_{Q}x_{R},}">

<p id="mwAQc">and because both equations are cubics, they must be the same polynomial up to a scalar. Then <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Equating_the_coefficients" title="Equating the coefficients" class="mw-redirect" id="mwAQg">equating the coefficients</a> of <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt144" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;x&apos;&apos;&lt;sup>2&lt;/sup>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>x</i><sup>2</sup></span> in both equations</p>

<p id="mwAQ0">and solving for the unknown <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt146" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAQ4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x&lt;sub>R&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>x<sub>R</sub></span>,</p>

<p id="mwARI"><span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt148" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwARM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"y&lt;sub>R&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>y<sub>R</sub></span> follows from the line equation</p>
<dl id="mwARQ"><dd id="mwARU"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt149" id="mwARY" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"y_R = y_P - s(x_P - x_R),"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle y_{R}=y_{P}-s(x_{P}-x_{R}),}">

<p id="mwARc">and this is an element of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt150" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwARg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"K"}},"i":0}}]}'>K</span>, because <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt151" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwARk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"s"}},"i":0}}]}'>s</span> is.</p>

<p id="mwARo">If <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt152" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwARs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;x&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;x&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>x<sub>P</sub></i> = <i>x<sub>Q</sub></i></span>, then there are two options: if <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt153" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwARw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; = −&apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y<sub>P</sub></i> = −<i>y<sub>Q</sub></i></span> (case <i id="mwAR0">3</i>), including the case where <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt154" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwAR4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; = 0"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y<sub>P</sub></i> = <i>y<sub>Q</sub></i> = 0</span> (case <i id="mwAR8">4</i>), then the sum is defined as 0; thus, the inverse of each point on the curve is found by reflecting it across the <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt155" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"x"}},"i":0}}]}'>x</span><span typeof="mw:Entity" id="mwASE"> </span>axis.</p>

<p id="mwASI">If <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt156" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>P&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;y&lt;sub>Q&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; ≠ 0"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>y<sub>P</sub></i> = <i>y<sub>Q</sub></i> ≠ 0</span>, then <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt157" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;Q&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;P&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>Q</i> = <i>P</i></span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt158" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASU" data-mw="{&quot;parts&quot;:[{&quot;template&quot;:{&quot;target&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;math&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;./Template:Math&quot;},&quot;params&quot;:{&quot;1&quot;:{&quot;wt&quot;:&quot;''R'' = (''x''&lt;sub>''R''&lt;/sub>, ''y''&lt;sub>''R''&lt;/sub>) = −(''P'' + ''P'') = −2''P'' = −2''Q''&quot;}},&quot;i&quot;:0}}]}"><i>R</i> = (<i>x</i><sub><i>R</i></sub>, <i>y</i><sub><i>R</i></sub>) = −(<i>P</i> + <i>P</i>) = −2<i>P</i> = −2<i>Q</i></span> (case <i id="mwASY">2</i> using <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt159" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASc" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"P"}},"i":0}}]}'>P</span> as <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt160" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwASg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"R"}},"i":0}}]}'>R</span>). The slope is given by the tangent to the curve at (<i id="mwASk">x</i><sub id="mwASo"><i id="mwASs">P</i></sub>, <i id="mwASw">y</i><sub id="mwAS0"><i id="mwAS4">P</i></sub>).</p>

<p id="mwATI">A more general expression for <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt162" id="mwATM" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"s"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle s}">
