    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle y^{2}+a_{1}xy+a_{3}y=x^{3}+a_{2}x^{2}+a_{4}x+a_{6}}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/f4c91626d1e5a1e25d3e4e3be4eba70f8df713c9" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; width:40.674ex; height:3.009ex;" alt="{\displaystyle y^{2}+a_{1}xy+a_{3}y=x^{3}+a_{2}x^{2}+a_{4}x+a_{6}}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwA9k">provided that the variety it defines is non-singular. If characteristic were not an obstruction, each equation would reduce to the previous ones by a suitable linear change of variables.</p>

<p id="mwA9o">One typically takes the curve to be the set of all points (<i id="mwA9s">x</i>,<i id="mwA9w">y</i>) which satisfy the above equation and such that both <i id="mwA90">x</i> and <i id="mwA94">y</i> are elements of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebraic_closure" title="Algebraic closure" id="mwA98">algebraic closure</a> of <i id="mwA-A">K</i>. Points of the curve whose coordinates both belong to <i id="mwA-E">K</i> are called <b id="mwA-I"><i id="mwA-M">K</i>-rational points</b>.</p>

<p id="mwA-Q">Many of the preceding results remain valid when the field of definition of <i id="mwA-U">E</i> is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Number_field" title="Number field" class="mw-redirect" id="mwA-Y">number field</a> <i id="mwA-c">K</i>, that is to say, a finite <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Field_extension" title="Field extension" id="mwA-g">field extension</a> of <b id="mwA-k">Q</b>. In particular, the group <i id="mwA-o">E(K)</i> of <i id="mwA-s">K</i>-rational points of an elliptic curve <i id="mwA-w">E</i> defined over <i id="mwA-0">K</i> is finitely generated, which generalizes the Mordell–Weil theorem above. A theorem due to <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loïc_Merel" title="Loïc Merel" id="mwA-4">Loïc Merel</a> shows that for a given integer <i id="mwA-8">d</i>, there are (<a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Up_to" title="Up to" id="mwA_A">up to</a> isomorphism) only finitely many groups that can occur as the torsion groups of <i id="mwA_E">E</i>(<i id="mwA_I">K</i>) for an elliptic curve defined over a number field <i id="mwA_M">K</i> of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Degree_of_a_field_extension" title="Degree of a field extension" id="mwA_Q">degree</a> <i id="mwA_U">d</i>. More precisely,<sup about="#mwt303" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-22" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-22"}}'><a href="#cite_note-22" id="mwA_Y"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwA_c"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_g">[</span>22<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwA_k">]</span></span></a></sup> there is a number <i id="mwA_o">B</i>(<i id="mwA_s">d</i>) such that for any elliptic curve <i id="mwA_w">E</i> defined over a number field <i id="mwA_0">K</i> of degree <i id="mwA_4">d</i>, any torsion point of <i id="mwA_8">E</i>(<i id="mwBAA">K</i>) is of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Order_(group_theory)" title="Order (group theory)" id="mwBAE">order</a> less than <i id="mwBAI">B</i>(<i id="mwBAM">d</i>). The theorem is effective: for <i id="mwBAQ">d</i> > 1, if a torsion point is of order <i id="mwBAU">p</i>, with <i id="mwBAY">p</i> prime, then</p>
<dl id="mwBAc"><dd id="mwBAg"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt304" id="mwBAk" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"p &lt; d^{3d^2}"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle p&lt;d^{3d^{2}}}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>p</mi>
        <mo>&lt;</mo>
        <msup>
          <mi>d</mi>
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mn>3</mn>
            <msup>
              <mi>d</mi>
              <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
                <mn>2</mn>
              </mrow>
            </msup>
          </mrow>
        </msup>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle p&lt;d^{3d^{2}}}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/81c6452e6068111976c7d67925f0124f348aca6f" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.671ex; margin-left: -0.089ex; width:8.323ex; height:3.343ex;" alt="{\displaystyle p&lt;d^{3d^{2}}}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwBAo">As for the integral points, <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siegel's_theorem_on_integral_points" title="Siegel's theorem on integral points" id="mwBAs">Siegel's theorem</a> generalizes to the following: Let <i id="mwBAw">E</i> be an elliptic curve defined over a number field <i id="mwBA0">K</i>, <i id="mwBA4">x</i> and <i id="mwBA8">y</i> the Weierstrass coordinates. Then there are only finitely many points of <i id="mwBBA">E(K)</i> whose <i id="mwBBE">x</i>-coordinate is in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_of_integers" title="Ring of integers" id="mwBBI">ring of integers</a> <i id="mwBBM">O</i><sub id="mwBBQ"><i id="mwBBU">K</i></sub>.</p>

<p id="mwBBY">The properties of the Hasse–Weil zeta function and the Birch and Swinnerton-Dyer conjecture can also be extended to this more general situation.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="11" id="mwBBc" aria-labelledby="Elliptic_curves_over_the_complex_numbers"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Elliptic_curves_over_the_complex_numbers">Elliptic curves over the complex numbers</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Elliptic curves over the complex numbers"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<span class="mw-empty-elt" about="#mwt305" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBBg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"Further","href":"./Template:Further"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Complex multiplication"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1368532237" about="#mwt306" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Module:Hatnote/styles.css"},"body":{"extsrc":""}}'/></span><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable" about="#mwt305" id="mwBBk">Further information: <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_multiplication" title="Complex multiplication">Complex multiplication</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb" id="mwBBo"><a href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lattice_torsion_points.svg" class="mw-file-description" id="mwBBs"><img resource="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/File:Lattice_torsion_points.svg" src="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Lattice_torsion_points.svg/330px-Lattice_torsion_points.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail" decoding="async" loading="lazy" srcset="//thumb.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d5/Lattice_torsion_points.svg/960px-Lattice_torsion_points.svg.png?utm_source=en.wikipedia.org&amp;utm_campaign=parser&amp;utm_content=thumbnail 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="1000" data-file-type="drawing" height="375" width="300" class="mw-file-element mw-file-upright" style="--mw-file-upright: 1.2" id="mwBBw"/></a><figcaption id="mwBB0">An elliptic curve over the complex numbers is obtained as a quotient of the complex plane by a lattice <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt307" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBB4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'>Λ</span>, here spanned by two fundamental periods <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt308" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBB8" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;ω&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>1&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>ω</i><sub>1</sub></span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt309" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBCA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;ω&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>2&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>ω</i><sub>2</sub></span>. The four-torsion is also shown, corresponding to the lattice <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt310" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBCE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"{{sfrac|1|4}}Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1354360663" about="#mwt311" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Sfrac/styles.css"}}'/><span class="sfrac"><span typeof="mw:Entity">⁠</span><span class="tion"><span class="num">1</span><span class="sr-only">/</span><span class="den">4</span></span><span typeof="mw:Entity">⁠</span></span>Λ</span> containing <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt312" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBCI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'>Λ</span>.</figcaption></figure>
<p id="mwBCM">The formulation of elliptic curves as the embedding of a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torus" title="Torus" id="mwBCQ">torus</a> in the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_projective_plane" title="Complex projective plane" id="mwBCU">complex projective plane</a> follows naturally from a curious property of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weierstrass's_elliptic_functions" title="Weierstrass's elliptic functions" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBCY">Weierstrass's elliptic functions</a>.  These functions and their first derivative are related by the formula</p>

<dl id="mwBCc"><dd id="mwBCg"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt313" id="mwBCk" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\wp&apos;(z)^2 = 4\\wp(z)^3 -g_2\\wp(z) - g_3"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \wp '(z)^{2}=4\wp (z)^{3}-g_{2}\wp (z)-g_{3}}">

          </mrow>
        </msup>
        <mo>=</mo>
        <mn>4</mn>
        <mi mathvariant="normal">℘<!-- ℘ --></mi>

        <mi>z</mi>

  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/591fdb589a978562eabce740e3baa8c2e9437c7c" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:30.189ex; height:3.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle \wp '(z)^{2}=4\wp (z)^{3}-g_{2}\wp (z)-g_{3}}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwBCo">Here, <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt314" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBCs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>2&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>g</i><sub>2</sub></span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt315" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBCw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>3&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>g</i><sub>3</sub></span> are constants; <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt316" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBC0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"℘(&apos;&apos;z&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'>℘(<i>z</i>)</span> is the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weierstrass_elliptic_function" title="Weierstrass elliptic function" id="mwBC4">Weierstrass elliptic function</a> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt317" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBC8" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"℘{{prime}}(&apos;&apos;z&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'>℘<span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.15em;">′</span>(<i>z</i>)</span> its derivative. It should be clear that this relation is in the form of an elliptic curve (over the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Complex_number" title="Complex number" id="mwBDA">complex numbers</a>). The Weierstrass functions are doubly periodic; that is, they are <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fundamental_pair_of_periods" title="Fundamental pair of periods" id="mwBDE">periodic</a> with respect to a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lattice_(group)" title="Lattice (group)" id="mwBDI">lattice</a> <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt318" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBDM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'>Λ</span>; in essence, the Weierstrass functions are naturally defined on a torus  <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt319" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBDQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;T&apos;&apos; = &apos;&apos;&apos;C&apos;&apos;&apos;/Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>T</i> = <b>C</b>/Λ</span>. This torus may be embedded in the complex projective plane by means of the map</p>

<dl id="mwBDU"><dd id="mwBDY"><span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt320" id="mwBDc" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"z \\mapsto \\left[1 : \\wp(z) : \\tfrac12\\wp&apos;(z)\\right]"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle z\mapsto \left[1:\wp (z):{\tfrac {1}{2}}\wp '(z)\right]}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>z</mi>
        <mo stretchy="false">↦<!-- ↦ --></mo>
        <mrow>
          <mo>[</mo>
          <mrow>
            <mn>1</mn>
            <mo>:</mo>
            <mi mathvariant="normal">℘<!-- ℘ --></mi>
            <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
            <mi>z</mi>
            <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
            <mo>:</mo>
            <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
              <mstyle displaystyle="false" scriptlevel="0">
                <mfrac>
                  <mn>1</mn>
                  <mn>2</mn>
                </mfrac>
              </mstyle>
            </mrow>
            <msup>
              <mi mathvariant="normal">℘<!-- ℘ --></mi>
              <mo>′</mo>
            </msup>
            <mo stretchy="false">(</mo>
            <mi>z</mi>

    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle z\mapsto \left[1:\wp (z):{\tfrac {1}{2}}\wp '(z)\right]}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/c351bf351c830acec700d97d49f2eeff421f8644" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -1.171ex; width:22.773ex; height:3.509ex;" alt="{\displaystyle z\mapsto \left[1:\wp (z):{\tfrac {1}{2}}\wp '(z)\right]}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwBDg">This map is a <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Group_isomorphism" title="Group isomorphism" id="mwBDk">group isomorphism</a> of the torus (considered with its natural group structure) with the chord-and-tangent group law on the cubic curve which is the image of this map.  It is also an isomorphism of <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riemann_surface" title="Riemann surface" id="mwBDo">Riemann surfaces</a> from the torus to the cubic curve, so topologically, an elliptic curve is a torus.  If the lattice <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt321" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBDs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'>Λ</span> is related by multiplication by a non-zero complex number <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt322" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBDw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"c"}},"i":0}}]}'>c</span> to a lattice <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt323" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBD0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;c&apos;&apos;Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>c</i>Λ</span>, then the corresponding curves are isomorphic. Isomorphism classes of elliptic curves are specified by the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J-invariant" title="J-invariant" id="mwBD4"><span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt324" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBD8" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"j"}},"i":0}}]}'>j</span>-invariant</a>.</p>

<p id="mwBEA">The isomorphism classes can be understood in a simpler way as well. The constants <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt325" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBEE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>2&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>g</i><sub>2</sub></span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt326" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBEI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>3&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>g</i><sub>3</sub></span>, called the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J-invariant" title="J-invariant" id="mwBEM">modular invariants</a>, are uniquely determined by the lattice, that is, by the structure of the torus. However, all real polynomials factorize completely into linear factors over the complex numbers, since the field of complex numbers is the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebraic_closure" title="Algebraic closure" id="mwBEQ">algebraic closure</a> of the reals. So, the elliptic curve may be written as</p>

<p id="mwBFA">with <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J-invariant" title="J-invariant" id="mwBFE"><span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt339" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"j"}},"i":0}}]}'>j</span>-invariant</a> <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt330" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;j&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;τ&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>j</i>(<i>τ</i>)</span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt331" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFQ" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;λ&apos;&apos;(&apos;&apos;τ&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>λ</i>(<i>τ</i>)</span> is sometimes called the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modular_lambda_function" title="Modular lambda function" id="mwBFU">modular lambda function</a>. For example, let <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt332" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFY" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;τ&apos;&apos; = 2&apos;&apos;i&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>τ</i> = 2<i>i</i></span>, then <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt333" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFc" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;λ&apos;&apos;(2&apos;&apos;i&apos;&apos;) = (−1 + {{sqrt|2}})&lt;sup>4&lt;/sup>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>λ</i>(2<i>i</i>) = (−1 + <span class="nowrap"><span typeof="mw:Entity">√</span><span style="border-top:1px solid; padding:0 0.1em;">2</span></span>)<sup>4</sup></span> which implies <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt334" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;{{prime}}&lt;sub>2&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>g</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.15em;">′</span><sub>2</sub></span>, <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt335" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;{{prime}}&lt;sub>3&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>g</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.15em;">′</span><sub>3</sub></span>, and therefore <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt336" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFo" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;{{prime}}&lt;sub>2&lt;/sub>{{su|p=3}} − 27&apos;&apos;g&apos;&apos;{{prime}}&lt;sub>3&lt;/sub>{{su|p=2}}"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>g</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.15em;">′</span><sub>2</sub><span class="nowrap"><span style="display:inline-block;margin-bottom:-0.3em;vertical-align:0.8em;line-height:1.2em;font-size:80%;text-align:left"><sup style="font-size:inherit;line-height:inherit;vertical-align:baseline">3</sup><br/><sub style="font-size:inherit;line-height:inherit;vertical-align:baseline"></sub></span></span> − 27<i>g</i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.15em;">′</span><sub>3</sub><span class="nowrap"><span style="display:inline-block;margin-bottom:-0.3em;vertical-align:0.8em;line-height:1.2em;font-size:80%;text-align:left"><sup style="font-size:inherit;line-height:inherit;vertical-align:baseline">2</sup><br/><sub style="font-size:inherit;line-height:inherit;vertical-align:baseline"></sub></span></span></span> of the formula above are all <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algebraic_numbers" title="Algebraic numbers" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBFs">algebraic numbers</a> if <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt337" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBFw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"τ"}},"i":0}}]}'>τ</span> involves an <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imaginary_quadratic_field" title="Imaginary quadratic field" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBF0">imaginary quadratic field</a>. In fact, it yields the integer <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt338" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBF4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;j&apos;&apos;(2&apos;&apos;i&apos;&apos;) = 66&lt;sup>3&lt;/sup> = {{val|287496}}"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>j</i>(2<i>i</i>) = 66<sup>3</sup> = <span class="nowrap"><span data-sort-value="7005287496000000000♠"></span>287<span style="margin-left:.25em;">496</span></span></span>. </p>

<p id="mwBF8">In contrast, the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modular_discriminant" title="Modular discriminant" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBGA">modular discriminant</a></p>

<p id="mwBGs">Note that the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uniformization_theorem" title="Uniformization theorem" id="mwBGw">uniformization theorem</a> implies that every <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compact_space" title="Compact space" id="mwBG0">compact</a> Riemann surface of genus one can be represented as a torus. This also allows an easy understanding of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torsion_subgroup" title="Torsion subgroup" id="mwBG4">torsion points</a> on an elliptic curve: if the lattice <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt343" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBG8" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Λ"}},"i":0}}]}'>Λ</span> is spanned by the fundamental periods <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt344" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBHA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;ω&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>1&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>ω</i><sub>1</sub></span> and <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt345" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBHE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"&apos;&apos;ω&apos;&apos;&lt;sub>2&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'><i>ω</i><sub>2</sub></span>, then the <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt346" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBHI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"n"}},"i":0}}]}'>n</span>-torsion points are the (equivalence classes of) points of the form</p>

<p id="mwBHY">for integers <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt348" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBHc" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"a"}},"i":0}}]}'>a</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt349" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBHg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"b"}},"i":0}}]}'>b</span> in the range <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt350" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBHk" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"0 ≤ (&apos;&apos;a&apos;&apos;, &apos;&apos;b&apos;&apos;) &lt; &apos;&apos;n&apos;&apos;"}},"i":0}}]}'>0 ≤ (<i>a</i>, <i>b</i>) &lt; <i>n</i></span>.</p>

<p id="mwBIc"><span class="texhtml " about="#mwt355" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBIg" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"M(&apos;&apos;w&apos;&apos;, &apos;&apos;z&apos;&apos;)"}},"i":0}}]}'>M(<i>w</i>, <i>z</i>)</span> is the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arithmetic–geometric_mean" title="Arithmetic–geometric mean" id="mwBIk">arithmetic–geometric mean</a> of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt356" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBIo" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"w"}},"i":0}}]}'>w</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt357" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBIs" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"z"}},"i":0}}]}'>z</span>. At each step of the arithmetic–geometric mean iteration, the signs of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt358" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBIw" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"z&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>z<sub>n</sub></span> arising from the ambiguity of geometric mean iterations are chosen such that <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt359" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBI0" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"{{abs|&apos;&apos;w&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; − &apos;&apos;z&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;}} ≤ {{abs|&apos;&apos;w&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;z&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;}}"}},"i":0}}]}'><span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em; padding-right:0.1em;"><i>w<sub>n</sub></i> − <i>z<sub>n</sub></i></span><span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span> ≤ <span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em; padding-right:0.1em;"><i>w<sub>n</sub></i> + <i>z<sub>n</sub></i></span><span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span></span> where <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt360" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBI4" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"w&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>w<sub>n</sub></span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt361" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBI8" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"z&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>"}},"i":0}}]}'>z<sub>n</sub></span> denote the individual arithmetic mean and geometric mean iterations of <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt362" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBJA" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"w"}},"i":0}}]}'>w</span> and <span class="texhtml mvar" style="font-style:italic;" about="#mwt363" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBJE" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"mvar","href":"./Template:Mvar"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"z"}},"i":0}}]}'>z</span>, respectively. When <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt364" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBJI" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"{{abs|&apos;&apos;w&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; − &apos;&apos;z&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;}} = {{abs|&apos;&apos;w&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos; + &apos;&apos;z&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;}}"}},"i":0}}]}'><span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em; padding-right:0.1em;"><i>w<sub>n</sub></i> − <i>z<sub>n</sub></i></span><span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span> = <span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em; padding-right:0.1em;"><i>w<sub>n</sub></i> + <i>z<sub>n</sub></i></span><span typeof="mw:Entity">|</span></span>, there is an additional condition that <span class="texhtml " about="#mwt365" typeof="mw:Transclusion" id="mwBJM" data-mw='{"parts":[{"template":{"target":{"wt":"math","href":"./Template:Math"},"params":{"1":{"wt":"Im&lt;big>(&lt;/big>{{sfrac|&apos;&apos;z&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;|&apos;&apos;w&lt;sub>n&lt;/sub>&apos;&apos;}}&lt;big>)&lt;/big> > 0"}},"i":0}}]}'>Im<big>(</big><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1354360663" about="#mwt366" typeof="mw:Extension/templatestyles" data-mw='{"name":"templatestyles","attrs":{"src":"Sfrac/styles.css"}}'/><span class="sfrac"><span typeof="mw:Entity">⁠</span><span class="tion"><span class="num"><i>z<sub>n</sub></i></span><span class="sr-only">/</span><span class="den"><i>w<sub>n</sub></i></span></span><span typeof="mw:Entity">⁠</span></span><big>)</big> > 0</span>.<sup about="#mwt369" class="mw-ref reference" id="cite_ref-23" rel="dc:references" typeof="mw:Extension/ref" data-mw='{"name":"ref","attrs":{},"body":{"id":"mw-reference-text-cite_note-23"}}'><a href="#cite_note-23" id="mwBJQ"><span class="mw-reflink-text" id="mwBJU"><span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJY">[</span>23<span class="cite-bracket" id="mwBJc">]</span></span></a></sup></p>

<p id="mwBJg">Over the complex numbers, every elliptic curve has nine <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inflection_point" title="Inflection point" id="mwBJk">inflection points</a>. Every line through two of these points also passes through a third inflection point; the nine points and 12 lines formed in this way form a realization of the <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hesse_configuration" title="Hesse configuration" id="mwBJo">Hesse configuration</a>.</p>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="12" id="mwBJs" aria-labelledby="The_dual_isogeny"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="The_dual_isogeny">The dual isogeny</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: The dual isogeny"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<section data-mw-section-id="13" id="mwBLA" aria-labelledby="Construction_of_the_dual_isogeny"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading3"><h3 id="Construction_of_the_dual_isogeny">Construction of the dual isogeny</h3><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Construction of the dual isogeny"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>

<p id="mwBLE">Often only the existence of a dual isogeny is needed, but it can be explicitly given as the composition</p>

<p id="mwBLU">where  <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt379" id="mwBLY" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\operatorname{Div}^0"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \operatorname {Div} ^{0}}">

To do this, we need maps <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt380" id="mwBLg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"E \\to \\operatorname{Div}^0(E)"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle E\to \operatorname {Div} ^{0}(E)}">

  <semantics>

        <mi>P</mi>

        <mo stretchy="false">)</mo>
        <mo stretchy="false">→<!-- → --></mo>
        <mi>E</mi>
        <mo>.</mo>
      </mstyle>
    </mrow>
    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle E'\to \operatorname {Pic} ^{0}(E')\to \operatorname {Pic} ^{0}(E)\to E.}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/a9923189eccfcc871e92fc2e75dc6c336fd4ace8" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:32.249ex; height:3.176ex;" alt="{\displaystyle E'\to \operatorname {Pic} ^{0}(E')\to \operatorname {Pic} ^{0}(E)\to E.}"/></span></dd></dl>

<p id="mwBNY">Note that the relation <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt399" id="mwBNc" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"f \\circ \\hat{f} = [n]"}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle f\circ {\hat {f}}=[n]}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">
        <mi>f</mi>
        <mo>∘<!-- ∘ --></mo>
        <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
          <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
            <mover>
              <mi>f</mi>
              <mo stretchy="false">^<!-- ^ --></mo>

        <mi>n</mi>
        <mo stretchy="false">]</mo>
      </mstyle>

    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle f\circ {\hat {f}}=[n]}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/1b3abeb3d135c6c7ac30f660bc8eb29930af3d63" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:10.959ex; height:3.343ex;" alt="{\displaystyle f\circ {\hat {f}}=[n]}"/></span> also implies the conjugate relation <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt400" id="mwBNg" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\hat{f} \\circ f = [n]."}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle {\hat {f}}\circ f=[n].}">
  <semantics>
    <mrow class="MJX-TeXAtom-ORD">
      <mstyle displaystyle="true" scriptlevel="0">

    <annotation encoding="application/x-tex">{\displaystyle {\hat {f}}\circ f=[n].}</annotation>
  </semantics>
</math></span><img src="https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/media/math/render/svg/db9b4cff65c3c9027f5bc75a4aefebe3110726d9" class="mwe-math-fallback-image-inline mw-invert skin-invert" aria-hidden="true" style="vertical-align: -0.838ex; width:11.606ex; height:3.343ex;" alt="{\displaystyle {\hat {f}}\circ f=[n].}"/></span> Indeed, let  <span class="mwe-math-element mwe-math-element-inline" typeof="mw:Extension/math" about="#mwt401" id="mwBNk" data-mw='{"name":"math","attrs":{},"body":{"extsrc":"\\phi = \\hat{f} \\circ f."}}'><span class="mwe-math-mathml-a11y" style="display: none;"><math xmlns="http://www.w3.org/1998/Math/MathML" class="mathjax_ignore" alttext="{\displaystyle \phi ={\hat {f}}\circ f.}">

</section></section><section data-mw-section-id="14" id="mwBN4" aria-labelledby="Algorithms_that_use_elliptic_curves"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Algorithms_that_use_elliptic_curves">Algorithms that use elliptic curves</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Algorithms that use elliptic curves"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwBN8">Elliptic curves over finite fields are used in some <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cryptography" title="Cryptography" id="mwBOA">cryptographic</a> applications as well as for <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Integer_factorization" title="Integer factorization" id="mwBOE">integer factorization</a>. Typically, the general idea in these applications is that a known <a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Algorithm" title="Algorithm" id="mwBOI">algorithm</a> which makes use of certain finite groups is rewritten to use the groups of rational points of elliptic curves. For more see also:</p>
<ul id="mwBOM"><li id="mwBOQ"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic_curve_cryptography" title="Elliptic curve cryptography" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBOU">Elliptic curve cryptography</a>
<ul id="mwBOY"><li id="mwBOc"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dual_EC_DRBG" title="Dual EC DRBG" id="mwBOg">Dual EC DRBG</a> random number generator</li>
<li id="mwBOk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/EdDSA" title="EdDSA" id="mwBOo">EdDSA</a> digital signature algorithm</li>
<li id="mwBOs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic-curve_Diffie–Hellman" title="Elliptic-curve Diffie–Hellman" id="mwBOw">Elliptic-curve Diffie–Hellman</a> key exchange (ECDH)</li>
<li id="mwBO0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic_Curve_Digital_Signature_Algorithm" title="Elliptic Curve Digital Signature Algorithm" id="mwBO4">Elliptic curve digital signature algorithm</a> (ECDSA)</li>
<li id="mwBO8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supersingular_isogeny_key_exchange" title="Supersingular isogeny key exchange" id="mwBPA">Supersingular isogeny key exchange</a></li></ul></li>
<li id="mwBPE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elliptic_curve_primality_proving" title="Elliptic curve primality proving" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBPI">Elliptic curve primality proving</a></li>
<li id="mwBPM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lenstra_elliptic-curve_factorization" title="Lenstra elliptic-curve factorization" id="mwBPQ">Lenstra elliptic-curve factorization</a></li></ul>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="15" id="mwBPU" aria-labelledby="Alternative_representations_of_elliptic_curves"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="Alternative_representations_of_elliptic_curves">Alternative representations of elliptic curves</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Alternative representations of elliptic curves"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<ul id="mwBPY"><li id="mwBPc"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Doubling-oriented_Doche–Icart–Kohel_curve" title="Doubling-oriented Doche–Icart–Kohel curve" id="mwBPg">Doubling-oriented Doche–Icart–Kohel curve</a></li>
<li id="mwBPk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edwards_curve" title="Edwards curve" id="mwBPo">Edwards curve</a></li>
<li id="mwBPs"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hessian_form_of_an_elliptic_curve" title="Hessian form of an elliptic curve" id="mwBPw">Hessian curve</a></li>
<li id="mwBP0"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacobian_curve" title="Jacobian curve" id="mwBP4">Jacobian curve</a></li>
<li id="mwBP8"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Montgomery_curve" title="Montgomery curve" id="mwBQA">Montgomery curve</a></li>
<li id="mwBQE"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tripling-oriented_Doche–Icart–Kohel_curve" title="Tripling-oriented Doche–Icart–Kohel curve" id="mwBQI">Tripling-oriented Doche–Icart–Kohel curve</a></li>
<li id="mwBQM"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twists_of_curves" title="Twists of curves" class="mw-redirect" id="mwBQQ">Twisted curve</a></li>
<li id="mwBQU"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twisted_Edwards_curve" title="Twisted Edwards curve" id="mwBQY">Twisted Edwards curve</a></li>

</section><section data-mw-section-id="18" id="mwBfg" aria-labelledby="References"><div class="mw-heading mw-heading2"><h2 id="References">References</h2><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Elliptic_curve&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: References"><span>edit</span></a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></div>
<p id="mwBfk"><a rel="mw:WikiLink" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serge_Lang" title="Serge Lang" id="mwBfo">Serge Lang</a>, in the introduction to the book cited below, stated that "It is possible to write endlessly on elliptic curves. (This is not a threat.)" The following short list is thus at best a guide to the vast expository literature available on the theoretical, algorithmic, and cryptographic aspects of elliptic curves.</p>
